To do so, that permanent’s controller creates an N/N white Spirit creature token unless they put N +1/+1 counters on that permanent.\n \n701.63b If a permanent is instructed to endure 0, nothing happens. Harness\n \n701.64a “Harness [this permanent]” means “If this permanent isn’t harnessed, it becomes harnessed.”\n \n701.64b Harnessed is a designation that has no rules meaning other than to act as a marker that other spells and abilities can identify. Only permanents can be or become harnessed. Once a permanent becomes harnessed, it stays harnessed until it leaves the battlefield. Harnessed is neither an ability nor part of the permanent’s copiable values.\n \n701.65. Airbend\n \n701.65a Certain spells and abilities instruct a player to airbend one or more permanents and/or spells. To do so, that player exiles those objects. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0172szCDrFyhMXVxZHv4KUYi","type":"tool_result","content":"24:110. Permanents\n612:110. Permanents\n614:110.1. A permanent is a card or token on the battlefield. A permanent remains on the battlefield indefinitely. A card or token becomes a permanent as it enters the battlefield and it stops being a permanent as it’s moved to another zone by an effect or rule.\n616:110.2. A permanent’s owner is the same as the owner of the card that represents it (unless it’s a token; see rule 111.2). A permanent’s controller is, by default, the player under whose control it entered the battlefield. Every permanent has a controller.\n618:110.2a If an effect instructs a player to put an object onto the battlefield, that object enters the battlefield under that player’s control unless the effect states otherwise.\n620:110.2b If an effect causes a player to gain control of another player’s permanent spell, the first player controls the permanent that spell becomes, but the permanent’s controller by default is the player who put that spell onto the stack. (This distinction is relevant in multiplayer games; see rule 800.4c.)\n622:110.3. A nontoken permanent’s characteristics are the same as those printed on its card, as modified by any continuous effects. See rule 613, “Interaction of Continuous Effects.”\n624:110.4. There are six permanent types: artifact, battle, creature, enchantment, land, and planeswalker. Instant and sorcery cards can’t enter the battlefield and thus can’t be permanents. Some kindred cards can enter the battlefield and some can’t, depending on their other card types. See section 3, “Card Types.”\n626:110.4a The term “permanent card” is used to refer to a card that could be put onto the battlefield. Specifically, it means an artifact, battle, creature, enchantment, land, or planeswalker card.\n628:110.4b The term “permanent spell” is used to refer to a spell that will enter the battlefield as a permanent as part of its resolution. Specifically, it means an artifact, battle, creature, enchantment, or planeswalker spell.\n630:110.4c If a permanent somehow loses all its permanent types, it remains on the battlefield. It’s still a permanent.\n632:110.5. A permanent’s status is its physical state. There are four status categories, each of which has two possible values: tapped/untapped, flipped/unflipped, face up/face down, and phased in/phased out. Each permanent always has one of these values for each of these categories.\n634:110.5a Status is not a characteristic, though it may affect a permanent’s characteristics.\n636:110.5b Permanents enter the battlefield untapped, unflipped, face up, and phased in unless a spell or ability says otherwise.\n638:110.5c A permanent retains its status until a spell, ability, or turn-based action changes it, even if that status is not relevant to it.\n641:110.5d Only permanents have status. Cards not on the battlefield do not. Although an exiled card may be face down, this has no correlation to the face-down status of a permanent. Similarly, cards not on the battlefield are neither tapped nor untapped, regardless of their physical state.","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"d219dbd7-ac6f-4400-9a89-d2f48db1b349","uuid":"c16aa072-adc9-45bf-8f91-2fd733c252bd","timestamp":"2026-09-03T14:58:01.459Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"24:110. Defaults to `\"any\"` if omitted.\n\n- `\"you\"` — you control it\n- `\"any\"` — any player controls it (typically for targets)\n- `\"opponent\"` — an opponent controls it\n\n### `target`\n\nTargeting requirements per CR 114. Present only if the effect targets.\n\n- `count`: how many objects must/may be chosen (1, 2, etc.)\n- `optional`: whether this is \"up to N\" targeting\n\n### `restriction`\n\nList of constraints that filter the selectable set.\n\n- `{\"another\": true}` — another (not this permanent)\n- `{\"excludes_type\": \"<Type>\"}` — exclude permanents of that type\n- `{\"is_token\": true}` — must be a token\n\n## Examples\n\n### \"target creature\"\n\n```json\n{\n  \"class\": \"obj:type:creature\",\n  \"selector\": {\n    \"target\": {\"count\": 1}\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### \"up to two other target nonland permanents you control\"\n\n```json\n{\n  \"class\": \"obj:category:permanent\",\n  \"selector\": {\n    \"controller\": \"you\",\n    \"target\": {\"count\": 2, \"optional\": true},\n    \"restriction\": [\n      {\"another\": true},\n      {\"excludes_type\": \"Land\"}\n    ]\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### \"target artifact token\"\n\n```json\n{\n  \"class\": \"obj:type:artifact\",\n  \"selector\": {\n    \"target\": {\"count\": 1},\n    \"restriction\": [{\"is_token\": true}]\n  }\n}\n```\n\n## Anti-patterns\n\n❌ `obj:target-creature` — selector baked into concept name\n\n✅ `obj:type:creature` + `{\"target\": {\"count\": 1}}` — selector as edge data\n\nThe concept vocabulary contains only the *class* of object.
